Man aged 19 is referred by the GP with a 2-day history of productive cough with mucopurulent sputum, fevers at home, and breathlessness. Associated symptoms: sharp chest pain worse on inspiration and reduced appetite.

PMH: nil of note. Current smoker (22 cigarettes/day).

O/E: T 38.6 degC, HR 94 bpm, RR 27/min, BP 135/84 mmHg, SpO2 94% on room air. Focal coarse crackles with increased vocal resonance at the right mid zone.

CXR: right middle lobe consolidation. Bloods: WCC 17.4, CRP 308 mg/L, urea 5.1 mmol/L; blood cultures sent.

Assessment: community-acquired pneumonia (right middle lobe consolidation), CURB-65 = 0 (no criteria met) - moderate severity.

Plan: Admitted under the medical team: treated as moderate CAP with IV co-amoxiclav and clarithromycin; VTE prophylaxis and early mobilisation planned.

O/E: T 39.1 degC, HR 101 bpm, RR 21/min, BP 112/68 mmHg, SpO2 98% on room air. Persistent tachycardia, injected eyes, truncal rash; mild hepatomegaly.

CRP 304 mg/L, ferritin high, troponin mildly raised, BNP raised; COVID serology positive for past infection.

Assessment: paediatric inflammatory multisystem syndrome (MIS-C) - mild.

Plan: Admitted to paediatrics: IVIG 2 g/kg planned, cardiac echo, fluid restriction.

B524C0AD kawasaki disease3-5 childM White90-99cm10-19kg n/aSite Bfever rash conjunctival_injection red_cracked_lips strawberry_tongue swollen_hands_feet irritability
Paediatric ED: 3-year-old boy, parents reports 5 days of high temperature, an erythematous rash, and red eyes. Associated symptoms: red, cracked lips, a strawberry tongue, swollen, red hands and feet, and unusual irritability.

Background: Ex-full-term infant, immunisations up to date, no regular medications.

O/E: T 40.3 degC, HR 126 bpm, RR 32/min, BP 97/59 mmHg, SpO2 98% on room air. Bilateral non-exudative conjunctival injection; cracked red lips with strawberry tongue; polymorphous truncal rash; indurative oedema of the hands and feet; one 2.2 cm tender cervical node.

WCC 17.9, CRP 92 mg/L, ESR 91, platelets 608 (rising); echocardiogram booked - coronary dimensions normal so far.

Assessment: Kawasaki disease, complete form, day 9 of fever - IVIG needed within 10 days of onset.

Plan: Admitted to paediatrics: IVIG 2 g/kg over 12 hours plus aspirin 3 mg/kg; serial echocardiography for coronary aneurysms.
